- 2026-11-17 — **Fork `embed-records` converged after three reconciliation rounds; `[ev]` PR opened** on `embed/ev-finished-body`. Both designers rejected the brief's framing ("where do the records reach `finish`") and agree on one final state: the certificate is asked of each solid against itself; the finished body holds its records and the gate confirms each; a point or edge record inside one solid is a confirmed meeting place; between solids the census is the one reader, refusing at a deciding door only a meeting that door made. The PR edits `[topology.tiers]`' finished-body paragraph and this track's gate row, and carries fork-log row 284's recommendation half.
  - **Evidence behind the PR.** The three `Violated` readings on the benches are contacts between the four box copies of a product (`demos/tour`, `bench`); asked per solid no record is in play. The Boolean's `gate` holds its records (`Held`) and does not pass them to `finish`; `shell`, split and blend take a bare body and the evaluator carries the records after the door returns (`Shelled::carry_contacts`); a `⊔`'s records are minted by `record_at_rest` over the finished aggregate (`editor_core::product::gather_sources`, `step_import`'s `gate3`), at an assemble node on first read (`product::Census`).
  - **Decided here as sequencing, not put to Ev:** until the census has a lane for two curved solids near each other (`work/reachhold/census-cross-solid-curved-pairs-undecidable-on-shell-results.md`), a deciding door's between-solid meetings stay unasked, as today. The alternative, the certificate asking them minus the face pairs their records name, takes records on their word and gives those meetings two readers.
  - **The gate lane meanwhile** takes its census both ways (whole body, and per solid) and builds the per-solid ask as the branch's working shape; nothing between solids is built.
  - **Owed when Ev answers:** a note on REACHHOLD's log (the census inside a deciding door's finish waits on its row); a note on ATREST's three rows that close into "the finished body holds its records"; units for the records' move into `AtRestBody`, the confirmed meeting place, and the census at a deciding door.
  - **Off the question, from the designers, to file or fix:** `BooleanBody`'s doc cites `work/restread/census-cross-solid-…` for a row that is at `work/reachhold/`; whether `shell` over an operand with records keeps them is asked of the gate lane's census. (EMBED orchestrator)

One solid crossing itself always refuses, and an overlap a deciding door creates between its result's solids is that door's defect. The question put back to Ev, narrower than the PR body's: should an op over an assembly that already has a reported interference still build? If yes, the gate needs to tell an old overlap from a new one, which is what puts the finding on the body; if no, alternatives (a) or (b), and `[booleans]`' distribution sentence takes an exception. **The PR body's third decision was written from the mechanism and not from this question, which is why Ev had to ask twice;** the in-place rewrite leads with it. (EMBED orchestrator)
- 2026-11-18 — **Ev's third question on PR 7068's decision 3 (02:46Z): "how could − ever create a new overlap, or is this just about the discrete union case".** Answered: it cannot. `−`, `∩`, a split and an inward shell only remove material, so an overlap between their result's solids was in the operand; overlaps are born at a `⊔`, which reports, or where a door adds material to one piece of an assembly. So a removing door's gate need not read between solids descending from different operand pieces, and nothing need ride the body for it; the stored fact is needed only for a material-adding door to tell old from new. One designer had stated the fact; neither weighed that narrower rule as an option. **Both designers of fork `embed-records` are resumed on Ev's three questions** (recorded on `analysis/design-fork/embed-records`), each without the other's answer; the PR body's third decision is rewritten from what they return. (EMBED orchestrator)

- 2026-11-21 — **Ev's second comment on PR 7095 (20:06Z): "the margin is needed/used for things that must tolerate the geometry being eps off from real. is this one of those cases, where geometry that's eps off could cause issues?" Answered yes, and the strict-sign recommendation of an hour before is withdrawn (20:07Z).** If each face's surface is ε off over its reach r, its direction at the shared edge is off by about ε/r, so a wedge that opens by less than about 2ε at the faces' far side could really open the other way. The margin at the faces' reach is that tolerance, on curved faces as on flat ones. Checked in the tree this time before saying it: tier 3's check 4 already takes this margin at every edge ("bounded away from the ends by θ = ε/r", `edge_wedge`, levered at the faces' extent), at stations along the edge, and embedding is asked only after tier 3 passes (`AtRestBody::validate` on `embed/gate`). So the departure check's margin covers the stretches between stations, which is the designers' reason for it. Decision 2 is put back to Ev as "keep the margin, at the faces' reach", the designers' lean; decision 3 (the drum's order) is still open.
  - **The orchestrator's miss, the second of its kind on this PR:** a recommendation against three designers was posted on an inference from one sentence of Ev's ("could be using eps for something other than a maximum deviation") and on a fact said to be unchecked (how tier 3 reads the wedge). - 2026-11-22 — **`embed/verdict-read`: the design is in (23:54Z) and approved (23:56Z).** Tier 3 keeps, per edge, `EdgeFact { class: Option<EdgeClass>, extent: Lever }` (class = a crease with its turn, a seam, an end; `None` where check 4 exempts the edge), per face its singular vertices, per vertex-alone face pair an extent; `AtRestBody` holds them. The question takes a `Geometric` (a body with its facts; no public constructor): from a finished body, or from `topo::geometric`, which runs tier 2, the session box and the local battery on an unfinished one and refuses typed if they fail, as PR 6893 had it. The class decides which separators run (the other face's carrier only across a crease, its sign held to the verdict's turn; nothing first-order across an end, which refuses `SecondOrder` at once). The cell reads `(|s_X| + |s_Y|) · g` times the extent against the band, `g` the ratio of the separator's least to greatest gradient norm over the pair (1 for a plane and for every carrier as built; checked for the graph).
  - **Rulings.** The run of a face is the supremum over the face of the distance from the shared locus, the pair's extent the lesser run, and every reading built is a lower bound of that. So a face whose whole boundary lies on the edge's own conic (every disc on its rim, a spherical cap), which boundary points read as 0, is read by a spanning bound: a one-loop face on one conic meets the conic's axis, hence runs at least its least radius; "one conic" by `one_conic` as PR 7328 leaves it, no second equality. Reading such a face from the edge's middle (2r for a disc) is more than the true r and is ruled out. Each face's own side is its sine's enclosure sign, unbanded, and the banded quantity is the sum: the ruling's "rates summed", pinned three ways. The kept lever crosses to the engine's scalar by a certified lift read from below (ONEROUND's doors apply). The cavity gate pays the local battery once more per op until EMBEDGATE retires it; the cost is to be measured.
  - **Conditions.** No body that holds on remeter's head may refuse for a loose bound (a spline edge's cover, a whole circle read for a short arc) without coming here first; every valid-looking body that newly refuses is listed by name with its narrower face, that face's run and the summed rate, split into truly-in-band, loose bound, and no class; that list comes here at the first full run, since it is where the owner's "less sure there won't be some issue later" would show. Predicted by the lane: the curling sliver reads 1e-11 and its flat twin 5e-12 (both refuse; check 4 passes both), the cone through its axis and the capsule's seam hold, contact9's sector ∩ reads 500ε once presented. (EMBED orchestrator)
